<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We give two finite-circle constructions of binary strings whose least <i>ℓ</i><sub>1</sub> distortion is $`\exp(\Omega(\sqrt{\log d\,\log\log d}))`$, where <i>d</i> bounds their length. Both constructions supply words of one common length for every sufficiently large cap. Two direct binary coding arguments transfer the constructions with absolute distortion and logarithmic block width. We also develop the overlapping-substring method of Ostrovsky and Rabani into a complete finite histogram embedding at the same exponential scale, uniformly over all finite alphabets and all words of length at most <i>d</i>, including the empty word.

- **C1** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): There are absolute constants $c,C>0$ and an integer $d_0$ such that, for every integer $d\geq d_0$ and every finite alphabet $\Sigma$ with $\|\Sigma\|\geq2$, $$\label{eq:main} \exp\!\bigl(c\sqrt{\log d\,\log\log d}\bigr) \leq E_\Sigma(d)\leq \exp\!\bigl(C\sqrt{\log d\,\log\log d}\bigr).$$ Each of our two lower constructions supplies a binary subset whose words have one common length at most $d$ and whose least $\ell_1$ distortion satisfies the lower bound. The upper bound is realized by a deterministic finite-dimensional map.
  - Explanation: Identifies the logarithmic scale of the least distortion uniformly over finite alphabets, with same-length binary witnesses and a deterministic upper map.
- **C2** (bound, [source](#stmt-pre-metric)): For all finite words $x,y$, $$q(x,y)\leq\operatorname{ED}(x,y)\leq2q(x,y),\qquad \operatorname{ED}(x,y)\leq \|x\|+\|y\|-2\operatorname{LCS}(x,y)\leq2\operatorname{ED}(x,y).$$ The middle expression in the second comparison is the insertion–deletion distance. In particular, $r_n\leq\operatorname{ED}\leq2r_n$ at equal length.
  - Explanation: Compares ordinary edit distance with subsequence deficit and insertion–deletion distance, including unequal lengths.
- **C3** (bound, [source](#stmt-pre-tagged-rows)): Let $R,L\geq1$ and $t\geq2$ be integers, and let $\Gamma_1,\ldots,\Gamma_t$ be pairwise disjoint alphabets. Each of two words consists of $R$ rows, each containing in order a block in $\Gamma_i^L$ for $i=1,\ldots,t$. Thus every matched pair preserves its child index. The common length is $N=RtL$. Fix an increasing matching with $E$ unmatched positions on each side. Suppose every nonsplit source row loses at least $AtL$ source positions, where $0\leq A\leq1$; rows with no matches are included in this hypothesis. Then $$\label{pre:row-bound} E\geq A\left(N-\frac{tE}{t-1}\right),\qquad E\geq\frac{AN}{1+tA/(t-1)}.$$
  - Explanation: Transfers losses in nonsplit tagged rows to a global deficit by charging disjoint unmatched target intervals for split rows.
- **C4** (bound, [source](#stmt-mask-cheap)): For every $0\leq\ell\leq h$, every $v\in G^{r_\ell}$, and every $c\in C_\ell$, $$\label{mask:cheap-subgroup} \operatorname{ED}(S_\ell(v),S_\ell(v+c))\leq\frac{2\ell L_\ell}{R}.$$ At the top level, for every leaf $i$ and $1\leq a\leq M/2$, $$\label{mask:cheap-leaf} \operatorname{ED}(S_h(v),S_h(v+e_i(a/M)))\leq\frac{2aL_h}{Mr_h}.$$ Both estimates hold at every base state $v$.
  - Explanation: Controls subgroup shifts and single-leaf shifts at every base state.
- **C5** (bound, [source](#stmt-mask-separation)): For every $0\leq\ell\leq h$, $v\in G^{r_\ell}$, and $\theta\in G$, $$\label{mask:separation-bound} r_{L_\ell}(S_\ell(v),S_\ell(v+\theta\mathbf1)) \geq\frac{2^{-\ell}}4L_\ell D_\ell(\theta).$$
  - Explanation: Transfers the scalar phase cost to a deficit lower bound under arbitrary alignments.
- **C6** (bound, [source](#stmt-mask-reset)): For all sufficiently large $h$, $$\label{mask:reset-bound} D_h(1/2)\geq4^{-h}h^{-h/2-O(\sqrt h)-4h/100},$$ with an absolute implicit constant.
  - Explanation: Keeps the half-turn phase costly by resetting accumulated denominator factors during descent.
- **C7** (bound, [source](#stmt-pre-cuts)): For $f:X\to\ell_1$ with $X$ finite, there are nonnegative coefficients $a_A$ on the nonempty proper subsets of $X$ such that $$\\|f(x)-f(y)\\|_1=\sum_A a_A\delta_A(x,y),\qquad \sum_Aa_A\leq\sum_{\{x,y\}\subset X}\\|f(x)-f(y)\\|_1.$$
  - Explanation: Represents finite-domain sequence-space distances by nonnegative cut weights, including a summability bound.
- **C8** (bound, [source](#stmt-pre-weighted-circle)): Let $H\leq V$, let $w=(1/2,\ldots,1/2)\in V$, and suppose $B>0$ satisfies $$\label{pre:branching-premise} \chi_\alpha\|_H=1,\quad \chi_\alpha(w)\ne1 \quad\Longrightarrow\quad T(\alpha)\geq B.$$ For every $F:V\to\ell_1$ there are nonnegative weights $\omega_\alpha$ with $$\label{pre:fourier} \Delta_F(a)=\sum_\alpha\omega_\alpha\|1-\chi_\alpha(a)\|^2 \qquad(a\in V).$$ Writing $H^\perp=\{\alpha:\chi_\alpha\|_H=1\}$, these weights satisfy $$\begin{aligned} \sum_{\alpha\notin H^\perp}\omega_\alpha &=\tfrac12\mathbb E_{u\in H}\Delta_F(u),\label{pre:off-mass}\\ 4\sum_\alpha\omega_\alpha T(\alpha) &\leq\sum_{i=1}^r\sum_{a\in\mathcal D_M} \frac Ma\Delta_F((a/M)e_i).\label{pre:weighted-mass} \end{aligned}$$ Consequently $$\label{pre:displacement} \Delta_F(w)\leq2\mathbb E_{u\in H}\Delta_F(u) +\frac1B\sum_{i=1}^r\sum_{a\in\mathcal D_M} \frac Ma\Delta_F((a/M)e_i).$$
  - Explanation: Bounds averaged half-turn displacement using subgroup shifts and dyadic single-coordinate shifts under a frequency threshold.
- **C9** (bound, [source](#stmt-mask-branching)): If $\chi_\alpha\|_{C_h}=1$ and $\chi_\alpha(w)\ne1$, then $$\label{mask:weight} \sum_{i=1}^r\|\alpha_i\|\geq B.$$
  - Explanation: Forces characters that annihilate the translation subgroup and detect the half-turn to have large total leaf frequency.
- **C10** (theorem, [source](#stmt-code-two-proofs)): Let $\mathcal A$ be a finite alphabet with $A\geq2$ letters. Each of the two constructions below gives an integer width $w\geq1$ with $w=O(\log(2A))$, an absolute constant $a>0$, and a map $c:\mathcal A\to\{0,1\}^w$ such that, simultaneously for every integer $L\geq0$ and all $x,y\in\mathcal A^L$, $$\label{p2:eq:log-code-deficit} a w r_L(x,y)\leq r_{wL}(c(x),c(y))\leq w r_L(x,y).$$ Here $c$ extends to words by concatenation. For arbitrary finite words, $$\label{p2:eq:log-code-upper} \operatorname{ED}(c(x),c(y))\leq w\operatorname{ED}(x,y).$$ The code and its width depend only on $\mathcal A$, not on $L$.
  - Explanation: Provides binary substitutions with logarithmic block width and absolute deficit loss at all equal input lengths; the expansion bound also covers unequal lengths.
- **C11** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-exc-small-cost)): For $0\le\ell\le h$, put $$F_\ell=m^{-\ell/4-100m^{3/4}}.$$ For all sufficiently large $m$, the following holds for every $0\le\ell\le h$. If $z\in\mathbb T$ is nonzero, has finite order $q\le m^{10}$, and satisfies $\gcd(q,\prod_{a=1}^{\ell}P_a)=1$, then $$\label{eq:exc-phase-lower} \rho_\ell(z)\ge F_\ell.$$ The empty product in the case $\ell=0$ is one.
  - Explanation: Keeps nonzero small-order phases costly when their orders are coprime to the available level primes.
- **C12** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-exc-alignment)): For every node $a$ at height $0\le\ell\le h$, every $v\in G$, and every $\theta,z\in L^{-1}\mathbb Z/\mathbb Z$, $$\label{eq:exc-word-separation} r_{N_\ell}\bigl(W_a(v,\theta),W_a(v,\theta+z)\bigr) \ge N_\ell 3^{-\ell}\rho_\ell(z).$$
  - Explanation: Transfers recursive phase cost to tagged-word separation under arbitrary alignments.
- **C13** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-exc-annihilator)): If $\xi\in H^\perp$ and $\chi_\xi(w_*)\ne1$, then $$\label{eq:exc-annihilator-threshold} T(\xi)\ge K,\qquad K=m^{3h/4}.$$
  - Explanation: Forces annihilating characters that detect the half-turn to have large total leaf frequency in the excluded-coordinate construction.
- **C14** (bound, [source](#stmt-assembly-inversion)): Let $c_0,C_0>0$ be fixed. Suppose that for every sufficiently large integer $k$ there is an integer $n_k\geq1$ and a finite subset of $\{0,1\}^{n_k}$ whose least $\ell_1$ distortion is at least $\exp(c_0k\log k)$, where $\log n_k\leq C_0k^2\log k$. Then there are constants $c>0$ and $d_0$, depending only on $c_0,C_0$ and the starting threshold, such that for every integer $d\geq d_0$ a binary subset of one length at most $d$ has distortion at least $\exp(c\sqrt{\log d\,\log\log d})$.
  - Explanation: Chooses a construction parameter directly from a sufficiently large cap, without requiring comparison of successive witness lengths.
- **C15** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-uniform-upper)): **Theorem 15** (Uniform upper bound). *There are absolute constants $C>0$ and $d_0\ge3$ such that, for every finite alphabet $\Sigma$ with $\|\Sigma\|\ge2$ and every integer $d\ge d_0$, there are an integer $M\ge1$ and a deterministic map $F:\Sigma^{\le d}\longrightarrow\ell_1^M$ satisfying $$\operatorname{ED}(x,y)\le \\|F(x)-F(y)\\|_1 \le \exp\!\bigl(C\sqrt{\log d\,\log\log d}\bigr) \operatorname{ED}(x,y) \qquad(x,y\in\Sigma^{\le d}).$$ Here $\ell_1^M$ is real, and both $C$ and $d_0$ are independent of $\Sigma$.*
  - Explanation: Constructs a deterministic finite-dimensional noncontracting map with alphabet-independent expansion, including all shorter words.
- **C16** (bound, [source](#stmt-upper-metric)): Let $\Delta(x,y)$ be the minimum number of insertions and deletions when substitutions are forbidden. For all finite words, $$\Delta(x,y)=\|x\|+\|y\|-2\operatorname{LCS}(x,y),\qquad \operatorname{ED}(x,y)\le\Delta(x,y)\le2\operatorname{ED}(x,y),$$ and $$\label{upper:unequal-comparison} q(x,y)\le\operatorname{ED}(x,y)\le2q(x,y).$$ In particular, for $\|x\|=\|y\|=n$, $$\label{upper:deficit-comparison} r_n(x,y)=\tfrac12\Delta(x,y),\qquad r_n(x,y)\le\operatorname{ED}(x,y)\le2r_n(x,y).$$
  - Explanation: Relates subsequence deficit, ordinary edit distance, and insertion–deletion distance under the stated unit costs.
- **C17** (bound, [source](#stmt-upper-move)): Two words of length $n\ge0$ can be joined by exactly $r_n(x,y)$ moves, each deleting one symbol and inserting one symbol while preserving length $n$. For a single move, restrictions to any common interval of $a$ positions have deficit at most one, and hence edit distance at most two.* *For a fixed partition of the positions into nonempty consecutive blocks, at most two blocks contain a deletion or insertion endpoint. In any other block of length $m$, the two subwords agree, or the prefix of length $m-1$ of one agrees with the suffix of length $m-1$ of the other.
  - Explanation: Decomposes equal-length comparisons into deficit-many moves and controls their restrictions to intervals and blocks.
- **C18** (bound, [source](#stmt-upper-padding)): For equal-length words $u,v$ of length $m$ and any common suffix $z$, $$\label{upper:common-suffix} r_{m+\|z\|}(uz,vz)=r_m(u,v).$$ If $\ast$ occurs in neither $x$ nor $y$, and $N\ge\max(\|x\|,\|y\|)$, then, writing $\widehat x=x\ast^{N-\|x\|}$ and $\widehat y=y\ast^{N-\|y\|}$, $$\label{upper:fresh-padding} \operatorname{LCS}(\widehat x,\widehat y) =\operatorname{LCS}(x,y)+N-\max(\|x\|,\|y\|),\qquad r_N(\widehat x,\widehat y)=q(x,y).$$ The second identity includes empty inputs.
  - Explanation: Preserves deficit under common suffixes and identifies fresh-symbol padding deficit with the unequal-length deficit.
- **C19** (bound, [source](#stmt-upper-partitions)): Let $X$ be finite and $\rho(u,v)=\\|f(u)-f(v)\\|_1$ for a map $f:X\to\ell_1$ into real sequence space. For every $\alpha>0$ there is a probability distribution with finite support on partitions $\pi$ of $X$ such that $$\label{upper:collision} \Pr[\pi(u)=\pi(v)]=e^{-\alpha\rho(u,v)}.$$ Here $\pi(u)$ denotes the part containing $u$. For a finite list $L$ in $X$, let $H_L^\pi$ be its count vector on the parts of $\pi$, with repetitions counted. Then $$\label{upper:histogram-map} \mathcal H_\alpha(L)=\bigoplus_\pi \Pr[\pi]\,H_L^\pi$$ is a deterministic finite-dimensional map whose distance equals $\mathbb E\\|H_L^\pi-H_{L'}^\pi\\|_1$.
  - Explanation: Constructs finite-support random partitions with an exact exponential collision probability and a deterministic expected histogram map.
- **C20** (bound, [source](#stmt-upper-histogram)): Let $s\ge1$ be an integer and let $L,L'$ be lists of size $s$ in the set of Lemma <a href="#upper:partitions" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="upper:partitions">19</a>, and set $T=\mathbb E\\|H_L^\pi-H_{L'}^\pi\\|_1$. If every cross-pair has $\rho$-distance at least $a$, then $$\label{upper:histogram-lower} T\ge2s(1-s^2e^{-\alpha a}).$$ If a pairing of list occurrences leaves $t$ occurrences unpaired on each side, then $$\label{upper:coupling} T\le2t+2\sum_{(u,v)\ {\rm paired}}(1-e^{-\alpha\rho(u,v)}) \le2t+2\alpha\sum_{(u,v)\ {\rm paired}}\rho(u,v).$$ Writing $P_L^\pi=H_L^\pi/s$ and $\operatorname{TV}(P,Q)=\tfrac12\\|P-Q\\|_1$, the exact normalizations are $$\label{upper:normalizations} \\|H_L^\pi-H_{L'}^\pi\\|_1 =s\\|P_L^\pi-P_{L'}^\pi\\|_1 =2s\,\operatorname{TV}(P_L^\pi,P_{L'}^\pi).$$
  - Explanation: Bounds expected histogram distances through cross-pair separation and occurrence pairings, with exact probability-histogram normalization.

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-pre-cuts",
        "exact_quote": "For $f:X\\to\\ell_1$ with $X$ finite, there are nonnegative coefficients $a_A$ on the nonempty proper subsets of $X$ such that $$\\|f(x)-f(y)\\|_1=\\sum_A a_A\\delta_A(x,y),\\qquad\n \\sum_Aa_A\\leq\\sum_{\\{x,y\\}\\subset X}\\|f(x)-f(y)\\|_1.$$",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-pre-weighted-circle",
        "exact_quote": "Let $H\\leq V$, let $w=(1/2,\\ldots,1/2)\\in V$, and suppose $B>0$ satisfies $$\\label{pre:branching-premise}\n \\chi_\\alpha|_H=1,\\quad \\chi_\\alpha(w)\\ne1\n \\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad T(\\alpha)\\geq B.$$ For every $F:V\\to\\ell_1$ there are nonnegative weights $\\omega_\\alpha$ with $$\\label{pre:fourier}\n \\Delta_F(a)=\\sum_\\alpha\\omega_\\alpha|1-\\chi_\\alpha(a)|^2\n \\qquad(a\\in V).$$ Writing $H^\\perp=\\{\\alpha:\\chi_\\alpha|_H=1\\}$, these weights satisfy $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\sum_{\\alpha\\notin H^\\perp}\\omega_\\alpha\n   &=\\tfrac12\\mathbb E_{u\\in H}\\Delta_F(u),\\label{pre:off-mass}\\\\\n 4\\sum_\\alpha\\omega_\\alpha T(\\alpha)\n   &\\leq\\sum_{i=1}^r\\sum_{a\\in\\mathcal D_M}\n        \\frac Ma\\Delta_F((a/M)e_i).\\label{pre:weighted-mass}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Consequently $$\\label{pre:displacement}\n \\Delta_F(w)\\leq2\\mathbb E_{u\\in H}\\Delta_F(u)\n +\\frac1B\\sum_{i=1}^r\\sum_{a\\in\\mathcal D_M}\n        \\frac Ma\\Delta_F((a/M)e_i).$$",
        "source_id": "canonical:PM-099.2"
      },
      "evidence": [

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-code-two-proofs",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathcal A$ be a finite alphabet with $A\\geq2$ letters. Each of the two constructions below gives an integer width $w\\geq1$ with $w=O(\\log(2A))$, an absolute constant $a>0$, and a map $c:\\mathcal A\\to\\{0,1\\}^w$ such that, simultaneously for every integer $L\\geq0$ and all $x,y\\in\\mathcal A^L$, $$\\label{p2:eq:log-code-deficit}\n a w r_L(x,y)\\leq r_{wL}(c(x),c(y))\\leq w r_L(x,y).$$ Here $c$ extends to words by concatenation. For arbitrary finite words, $$\\label{p2:eq:log-code-upper}\n \\operatorname{ED}(c(x),c(y))\\leq w\\operatorname{ED}(x,y).$$ The code and its width depend only on $\\mathcal A$, not on $L$.",
        "source_id": "canonical:PM-099.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-code-two-proofs",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathcal A$ be a finite alphabet with $A\\geq2$ letters. For arbitrary finite words, $$\\label{p2:eq:log-code-upper}\n \\operatorname{ED}(c(x),c(y))\\leq w\\operatorname{ED}(x,y).$$ The code and its width depend only on $\\mathcal A$, not on $L$.",
          "source_id": "canonical:PM-099.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $\\mathcal A$ be a finite alphabet with $A\\geq2$ letters.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-exc-small-cost",
        "exact_quote": "For $0\\le\\ell\\le h$, put $$F_\\ell=m^{-\\ell/4-100m^{3/4}}.$$ For all sufficiently large $m$, the following holds for every $0\\le\\ell\\le h$. If $z\\in\\mathbb T$ is nonzero, has finite order $q\\le m^{10}$, and satisfies $\\gcd(q,\\prod_{a=1}^{\\ell}P_a)=1$, then $$\\label{eq:exc-phase-lower}\n \\rho_\\ell(z)\\ge F_\\ell.$$ The empty product in the case $\\ell=0$ is one.",

      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-assembly-inversion",
        "exact_quote": "Let $c_0,C_0>0$ be fixed. Suppose that for every sufficiently large integer $k$ there is an integer $n_k\\geq1$ and a finite subset of $\\{0,1\\}^{n_k}$ whose least $\\ell_1$ distortion is at least $\\exp(c_0k\\log k)$, where $\\log n_k\\leq C_0k^2\\log k$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-uniform-upper",
        "exact_quote": "There are absolute constants $C>0$ and $d_0\\ge3$ such that, for every finite alphabet $\\Sigma$ with $|\\Sigma|\\ge2$ and every integer $d\\ge d_0$, there are an integer $M\\ge1$ and a deterministic map $F:\\Sigma^{\\le d}\\longrightarrow\\ell_1^M$ satisfying $$\\operatorname{ED}(x,y)\\le \\|F(x)-F(y)\\|_1\n \\le \\exp\\!\\bigl(C\\sqrt{\\log d\\,\\log\\log d}\\bigr)\n       \\operatorname{ED}(x,y)\n \\qquad(x,y\\in\\Sigma^{\\le d}).$$ Here $\\ell_1^M$ is real, and both $C$ and $d_0$ are independent of $\\Sigma$.",
        "source_id": "canonical:PM-099.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-uniform-upper",
          "exact_quote": "There are absolute constants $C>0$ and $d_0\\ge3$ such that, for every finite alphabet $\\Sigma$ with $|\\Sigma|\\ge2$ and every integer $d\\ge d_0$, there are an integer $M\\ge1$ and a deterministic map $F:\\Sigma^{\\le d}\\longrightarrow\\ell_1^M$ satisfying $$\\operatorname{ED}(x,y)\\le \\|F(x)-F(y)\\|_1\n \\le \\exp\\!\\bigl(C\\sqrt{\\log d\\,\\log\\log d}\\bigr)\n       \\operatorname{ED}(x,y)\n \\qquad(x,y\\in\\Sigma^{\\le d}).$$ Here $\\ell_1^M$ is real, and both $C$ and $d_0$ are independent of $\\Sigma$.",
          "source_id": "canonical:PM-099.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "**Theorem 15** (Uniform upper bound). *<span id=\"upper:uniform\" label=\"upper:uniform\"></span>There are absolute constants $C>0$ and $d_0\\ge3$ such that, for every finite alphabet $\\Sigma$ with $|\\Sigma|\\ge2$ and every integer $d\\ge d_0$, there are an integer $M\\ge1$ and a deterministic map $F:\\Sigma^{\\le d}\\longrightarrow\\ell_1^M$ satisfying $$\\operatorname{ED}(x,y)\\le \\|F(x)-F(y)\\|_1\n \\le \\exp\\!\\bigl(C\\sqrt{\\log d\\,\\log\\log d}\\bigr)\n       \\operatorname{ED}(x,y)\n \\qquad(x,y\\in\\Sigma^{\\le d}).$$ Here $\\ell_1^M$ is real, and both $C$ and $d_0$ are independent of $\\Sigma$.*",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-upper-metric",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\Delta(x,y)$ be the minimum number of insertions and deletions when substitutions are forbidden. For all finite words, $$\\Delta(x,y)=|x|+|y|-2\\operatorname{LCS}(x,y),\\qquad\n \\operatorname{ED}(x,y)\\le\\Delta(x,y)\\le2\\operatorname{ED}(x,y),$$ and $$\\label{upper:unequal-comparison}\n q(x,y)\\le\\operatorname{ED}(x,y)\\le2q(x,y).$$ In particular, for $|x|=|y|=n$, $$\\label{upper:deficit-comparison}\n r_n(x,y)=\\tfrac12\\Delta(x,y),\\qquad\n r_n(x,y)\\le\\operatorname{ED}(x,y)\\le2r_n(x,y).$$",
        "source_id": "canonical:PM-099.2"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-upper-move",
        "exact_quote": "Two words of length $n\\ge0$ can be joined by exactly $r_n(x,y)$ moves, each deleting one symbol and inserting one symbol while preserving length $n$. For a single move, restrictions to any common interval of $a$ positions have deficit at most one, and hence edit distance at most two.*\n\n*For a fixed partition of the positions into nonempty consecutive blocks, at most two blocks contain a deletion or insertion endpoint. In any other block of length $m$, the two subwords agree, or the prefix of length $m-1$ of one agrees with the suffix of length $m-1$ of the other.",
        "source_id": "canonical:PM-099.2"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-upper-padding",
        "exact_quote": "For equal-length words $u,v$ of length $m$ and any common suffix $z$, $$\\label{upper:common-suffix}\n r_{m+|z|}(uz,vz)=r_m(u,v).$$ If $\\ast$ occurs in neither $x$ nor $y$, and $N\\ge\\max(|x|,|y|)$, then, writing $\\widehat x=x\\ast^{N-|x|}$ and $\\widehat y=y\\ast^{N-|y|}$, $$\\label{upper:fresh-padding}\n \\operatorname{LCS}(\\widehat x,\\widehat y)\n =\\operatorname{LCS}(x,y)+N-\\max(|x|,|y|),\\qquad\n r_N(\\widehat x,\\widehat y)=q(x,y).$$ The second identity includes empty inputs.",
        "source_id": "canonical:PM-099.2"
      },

          "source_id": "canonical:PM-099.2"
        }
      ],

      "explanation": "Preserves deficit under common suffixes and identifies fresh-symbol padding deficit with the unequal-length deficit.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-upper-partitions",
        "exact_quote": "Let $X$ be finite and $\\rho(u,v)=\\|f(u)-f(v)\\|_1$ for a map $f:X\\to\\ell_1$ into real sequence space. For every $\\alpha>0$ there is a probability distribution with finite support on partitions $\\pi$ of $X$ such that $$\\label{upper:collision}\n \\Pr[\\pi(u)=\\pi(v)]=e^{-\\alpha\\rho(u,v)}.$$ Here $\\pi(u)$ denotes the part containing $u$. For a finite list $L$ in $X$, let $H_L^\\pi$ be its count vector on the parts of $\\pi$, with repetitions counted. Then $$\\label{upper:histogram-map}\n \\mathcal H_\\alpha(L)=\\bigoplus_\\pi \\Pr[\\pi]\\,H_L^\\pi$$ is a deterministic finite-dimensional map whose distance equals $\\mathbb E\\|H_L^\\pi-H_{L'}^\\pi\\|_1$.",
        "source_id": "canonical:PM-099.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-upper-partitions",

For every $\\alpha>0$ there is a probability distribution with finite support on partitions $\\pi$ of $X$ such that $$\\label{upper:collision}\n \\Pr[\\pi(u)=\\pi(v)]=e^{-\\alpha\\rho(u,v)}.$$ Here $\\pi(u)$ denotes the part containing $u$. For a finite list $L$ in $X$, let $H_L^\\pi$ be its count vector on the parts of $\\pi$, with repetitions counted. Then $$\\label{upper:histogram-map}\n \\mathcal H_\\alpha(L)=\\bigoplus_\\pi \\Pr[\\pi]\\,H_L^\\pi$$ is a deterministic finite-dimensional map whose distance equals $\\mathbb E\\|H_L^\\pi-H_{L'}^\\pi\\|_1$.",
          "source_id": "canonical:PM-099.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $X$ be finite and $\\rho(u,v)=\\|f(u)-f(v)\\|_1$ for a map $f:X\\to\\ell_1$ into real sequence space. For a finite list $L$ in $X$, let $H_L^\\pi$ be its count vector on the parts of $\\pi$, with repetitions counted. Then $$\\label{upper:histogram-map}\n \\mathcal H_\\alpha(L)=\\bigoplus_\\pi \\Pr[\\pi]\\,H_L^\\pi$$ is a deterministic finite-dimensional map whose distance equals $\\mathbb E\\|H_L^\\pi-H_{L'}^\\pi\\|_1$.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-upper-histogram",
        "exact_quote": "Let $s\\ge1$ be an integer and let $L,L'$ be lists of size $s$ in the set of Lemma <a href=\"#upper:partitions\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"upper:partitions\">19</a>, and set $T=\\mathbb E\\|H_L^\\pi-H_{L'}^\\pi\\|_1$. If every cross-pair has $\\rho$-distance at least $a$, then $$\\label{upper:histogram-lower}\n T\\ge2s(1-s^2e^{-\\alpha a}).$$ If a pairing of list occurrences leaves $t$ occurrences unpaired on each side, then $$\\label{upper:coupling}\n T\\le2t+2\\sum_{(u,v)\\ {\\rm paired}}(1-e^{-\\alpha\\rho(u,v)})\n   \\le2t+2\\alpha\\sum_{(u,v)\\ {\\rm paired}}\\rho(u,v).$$ Writing $P_L^\\pi=H_L^\\pi/s$ and $\\operatorname{TV}(P,Q)=\\tfrac12\\|P-Q\\|_1$, the exact normalizations are $$\\label{upper:normalizations}\n \\|H_L^\\pi-H_{L'}^\\pi\\|_1\n =s\\|P_L^\\pi-P_{L'}^\\pi\\|_1\n =2s\\,\\operatorname{TV}(P_L^\\pi,P_{L'}^\\pi).$$",
        "source_id": "canonical:PM-099.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-upper-histogram",
          "exact_quote": "Let $s\\ge1$ be an integer and let $L,L'$ be lists of size $s$ in the set of Lemma <a href=\"#upper:partitions\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"upper:partitions\">19</a>, and set $T=\\mathbb E\\|H_L^\\pi-H_{L'}^\\pi\\|_1$. If every cross-pair has $\\rho$-distance at least $a$, then $$\\label{upper:histogram-lower}\n T\\ge2s(1-s^2e^{-\\alpha a}).$$ If a pairing of list occurrences leaves $t$ occurrences unpaired on each side, then $$\\label{upper:coupling}\n T\\le2t+2\\sum_{(u,v)\\ {\\rm paired}}(1-e^{-\\alpha\\rho(u,v)})\n   \\le2t+2\\alpha\\sum_{(u,v)\\ {\\rm paired}}\\rho(u,v).$$ Writing $P_L^\\pi=H_L^\\pi/s$ and $\\operatorname{TV}(P,Q)=\\tfrac12\\|P-Q\\|_1$, the exact normalizations are $$\\label{upper:normalizations}\n \\|H_L^\\pi-H_{L'}^\\pi\\|_1\n =s\\|P_L^\\pi-P_{L'}^\\pi\\|_1\n =2s\\,\\operatorname{TV}(P_L^\\pi,P_{L'}^\\pi).$$",
          "source_id": "canonical:PM-099.2"
        }

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **ordinary edit distance** ([source](#pm-h-3)): For finite words $x,y$, let $\operatorname{ED}(x,y)$ be the least number of single-symbol insertions, deletions, and substitutions taking $x$ to $y$, with each operation costing one.
- **length-capped word space** ([source](#pm-h-3)): If $\Sigma$ is a finite alphabet with at least two symbols and $d\geq1$ is an integer, let $\Sigma^{\leq d}$ contain all words of length at most $d$, including the empty word.
- **distortion and its infimum** ([source](#pm-h-3)): For an injective map $f:\Sigma^{\leq d}\to\ell_1$, its distortion is $$\operatorname{dist}(f)= \left(\max_{x\ne y}\frac{\\|f(x)-f(y)\\|_1}{\operatorname{ED}(x,y)}\right) \left(\max_{x\ne y}\frac{\operatorname{ED}(x,y)}{\\|f(x)-f(y)\\|_1}\right), \qquad E_\Sigma(d)=\inf_f\operatorname{dist}(f).$$
- **equal-length subsequence deficit** ([source](#pm-h-8)): For equal-length words of length $n$, put $$r_n(x,y)=n-\operatorname{LCS}(x,y).$$
- **unequal-length subsequence deficit** ([source](#pm-h-8)): For arbitrary lengths put $q(x,y)=\max(\|x\|,\|y\|)-\operatorname{LCS}(x,y)$.
- **reset scalar phase cost** ([source](#mask:phase-cost)): To measure how much of a uniform phase survives those choices, define $$\label{mask:phase-cost} D_0(\theta)=\\|\theta\\|_{\mathbb R/\mathbb Z},\qquad D_\ell(\theta)=\min_z\frac1{t_\ell}\sum_{j\in J_\ell} D_{\ell-1}(\theta+\gamma_{\ell,j}(z)).$$
- **excluded-coordinate scalar phase cost** ([source](#eq:exc-rho)): The scalar cost of a phase after $\ell$ levels is $$\label{eq:exc-rho} \rho_0(z)=\\|z\\|_{\mathbb T},\qquad \rho_\ell(z)=\min_{s\bmod P_\ell} \frac1m\sum_{i=1}^m \rho_{\ell-1}\bigl(z+c_{\ell,i}(s)\bigr) \quad(1\le\ell\le h).$$
- **leaf frequency magnitude** ([source](#pm-h-25)): For each coordinate choose an integer representative of minimum absolute value, with either sign allowed in a tie, and put $$\label{eq:exc-frequency-size} T(\xi)=\sum_{a\in\Lambda}\|\xi_a\|.$$

| Q1 | least distortion lower scale | ≥ | $\exp\!\bigl(c\sqrt{\log d\,\log\log d}\bigr)$ | sufficiently large endpoint cap |
| Q2 | least distortion upper scale | ≤ | $\exp\!\bigl(C\sqrt{\log d\,\log\log d}\bigr)$ | sufficiently large endpoint cap |
| Q3 | ordinary edit distance | ≤ | $2q(x,y)$ | finite words |
| Q4 | matching deficit | ≥ | $\frac{AN}{1+tA/(t-1)}$ | tagged row parameters |
| Q5 | subgroup shift edit cost | ≤ | $\frac{2\ell L_\ell}{R}$ | reset construction |
| Q6 | uniform-shift deficit | ≥ | $\frac{2^{-\ell}}4L_\ell D_\ell(\theta)$ | reset construction |
| Q7 | half-turn scalar cost | ≥ | $4^{-h}h^{-h/2-O(\sqrt h)-4h/100}$ | asymptotic depth |
| Q8 | small-order phase threshold | = | $m^{-\ell/4-100m^{3/4}}$ | excluded-coordinate construction |
| Q9 | leaf frequency threshold | = | $m^{3h/4}$ | excluded-coordinate construction |
| Q10 | distortion lower bound | ≥ | $m^{m/4}$ | sufficiently large construction parameter |
| Q11 | partition rate | = | $\frac{4\log(s+1)}s$ | positive window scale |
| Q12 | total-variation partition rate | = | $\frac{\log(2s^2)}s$ | positive window scale |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **R2** [claim](#stmt-pre-metric) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pre-metric): Survivor counting and longest common subsequences -> Metric comparisons
- **R3** [claim](#stmt-pre-tagged-rows) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pre-tagged-rows): Charge disjoint target gaps created by split tagged rows -> Global alignment deficit
- **R4** [claim](#stmt-mask-cheap) / [proof](#proof-stmt-mask-cheap): Row shifts and leaf substitutions -> Cheap translations
- **R5** [claim](#stmt-mask-separation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-mask-separation): Induct on word height using the tagged-row deficit bound -> Word separation
- **R6** [claim](#stmt-mask-reset) / [proof](#proof-stmt-mask-reset): Follow nonzero phases and reset denominator factors -> Half-turn scalar separation
- **R7** [claim](#stmt-pre-cuts) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pre-cuts): Group coordinate threshold cuts and bound their total mass -> Finite cut representation
- **R8** [claim](#stmt-pre-weighted-circle) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pre-weighted-circle): Parseval expansion of cut indicators and dyadic frequency control -> Averaged displacement comparison
- **R9** [claim](#stmt-mask-branching) / [proof](#proof-stmt-mask-branching): Use prime congruences to force branching of nonzero node sums -> Frequency threshold
- **R10** [claim](#stmt-code-two-proofs) / [proof](#p2:sec:code-offset): Recover increasing letter matchings from binary interval separation or equal-index anchors -> Absolute-loss binary substitution
- **R11** [claim](#stmt-lem-exc-small-cost) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-exc-small-cost): Induct on height and control denominator accumulation along descent -> Small-order phase separation
- **R12** [claim](#stmt-lem-exc-alignment) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-exc-alignment): Induct through tagged rows under arbitrary alignments -> Excluded-coordinate word separation
- **R13** [claim](#stmt-lem-exc-annihilator) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-exc-annihilator): Use excluded-coordinate congruences to force nonzero leaf branching -> Excluded-coordinate frequency threshold
- **R14** [claim](#stmt-assembly-inversion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-assembly-inversion): Choose the integer construction parameter directly from the cap -> Same-length binary witnesses beneath every sufficiently large cap
- **R16** [claim](#stmt-upper-metric) / [proof](#proof-stmt-upper-metric): Count survivors and replace substitutions by insertion–deletion pairs -> Metric comparisons
- **R17** [claim](#stmt-upper-move) / [proof](#proof-stmt-upper-move): Protect a longest common subsequence and insert missing target symbols -> Move decomposition
- **R18** [claim](#stmt-upper-padding) / [proof](#proof-stmt-upper-padding): Match common final symbols and separate original symbols from fresh padding -> Padding identities
- **R19** [claim](#stmt-upper-partitions) / [proof](#proof-stmt-upper-partitions): Select threshold cuts independently and average all partition outcomes -> Finite deterministic histogram map
- **R20** [claim](#stmt-upper-histogram) / [proof](#proof-stmt-upper-histogram): Use a cross-collision union bound and occurrence couplings -> Histogram estimates
- **R15** [claim](#stmt-thm-uniform-upper) / [proof](#proof-stmt-upper-histogram-2): Recurse over overlapping windows and sum expected histograms -> Alphabet-uniform finite-dimensional embedding
- **M1** [claim](#mask:distortion) / [proof](#sec:mask-binary): Combine cheap shifts, half-turn separation and the weighted frequency inequality -> First same-length binary distortion obstruction
- **M2** [claim](#eq:exc-distortion-power) / [proof](#pm-h-26): Compare cut–Fourier energy with half-turn separation and bound the word length -> Excluded-coordinate same-length binary distortion obstruction
- **R1** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#sec:assembly): Restrict any ambient embedding to a hard binary subset and combine with the uniform upper embedding -> Matching logarithmic distortion scale for the capped word space.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **I-padding**: Fresh-symbol padding identifies the equal-length subsequence deficit with the original unequal-length deficit, including empty inputs.
- **I-metric**: The subsequence deficit and insertion–deletion distance bound unit-cost edit distance as stated in the metric comparisons.
- **I-uniform**: The deterministic finite-dimensional upper embedding covers the whole capped word domain with constants independent of the finite alphabet.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **A-fresh**: Pad with a symbol already present in an endpoint and retain the fresh-padding identity. -> Padding symbols can match original symbols, changing the longest common subsequence.
- **A-runtime**: Use the finite-dimensional upper embedding as an efficient production algorithm. -> A distortion guarantee gives no bound on construction time or target dimension.
- **A-length**: Apply the binary substitution lower bound to unequal-length inputs. -> The stated lower deficit bound is restricted to a common input length.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The fresh-symbol padding operation is a finite constructive component of the upper embedding. The code exercises its exact deficit identity and the finite-word metric comparisons; it does not implement or certify the asymptotic histogram embedding or either distortion obstruction.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON list of {x,y,N,star}; x,y are binary strings.
# PRECONDITIONS: star is a fresh single character; max(len(x),len(y)) <= N <= 5; endpoint lengths <= 3; batch size <= 1000.
# OUTPUTS: JSON rows [LCS,q,padded_LCS,padded_deficit,ED,insertion_deletion_distance].
# TIME: O(sum(N*N)) exact table operations over the batch.
# SPACE: O(N*N) table entries plus input/output storage.
# ARITHMETIC: exact integer arithmetic using python-flint fmpz for deficits.
# FAILURE: reject malformed or out-of-contract input with a nonzero exit.
import json, sys
from flint import fmpz

def tables(a,b):
    c=[[0]*(len(b)+1) for _ in range(len(a)+1)]
    e=[[0]*(len(b)+1) for _ in range(len(a)+1)]
    for i in range(len(a)+1): e[i][0]=i
    for j in range(len(b)+1): e[0][j]=j
    for i in range(1,len(a)+1):
        for j in range(1,len(b)+1):
            c[i][j]=c[i-1][j-1]+1 if a[i-1]==b[j-1] else max(c[i-1][j],c[i][j-1])
            e[i][j]=min(e[i-1][j]+1,e[i][j-1]+1,e[i-1][j-1]+(a[i-1]!=b[j-1]))
    return c[-1][-1],e[-1][-1]

def run(batch):
    if not isinstance(batch,list) or not 1<=len(batch)<=1000: raise ValueError('batch')
    result=[]
    for row in batch:
        if not isinstance(row,dict) or set(row)!={'x','y','N','star'}: raise ValueError('keys')
        x,y,N,star=(row[k] for k in ('x','y','N','star'))
        if not isinstance(x,str) or not isinstance(y,str) or any(c not in '01' for c in x+y): raise ValueError('words')
        if max(len(x),len(y))>3 or type(N) is not int or not max(len(x),len(y))<=N<=5: raise ValueError('cap')
        if not isinstance(star,str) or len(star)!=1 or star in x+y: raise ValueError('fresh')
        l,ed=tables(x,y)
        xp=x+star*(N-len(x)); yp=y+star*(N-len(y))
        padded,_=tables(xp,yp)
        q=int(fmpz(max(len(x),len(y)))-l)
        deficit=int(fmpz(N)-padded)
        delta=len(x)+len(y)-2*l
        result.append([l,q,padded,deficit,ed,delta])
    return result
print(json.dumps(run(json.load(sys.stdin)),separators=(',',':')))
```

    "language": "python",
    "mutants": [
      {
        "expected_failure": "Unequal-length boundary inputs disagree with exact enumeration.",
        "id": "M-short",
        "mutation": "Use the shorter endpoint length in the original deficit.",
        "observed_exit": 7,
        "receipt_sha256": "3a29680d2e0dc944f1e55cd49e86196245a37e6079b8ecc14fa294281bd3aeb7"
      },
      {
        "expected_failure": "Padded LCS and deficit disagree with exhaustive subsequence enumeration.",
        "id": "M-pad",
        "mutation": "Replace the fresh padding symbol by a binary symbol present in endpoints.",
        "observed_exit": 7,
        "receipt_sha256": "aaad29a8265fde8077fbf7ba18abd6a15ac86ac27c2fdbbfa0c177c79acdd7e0"
      },
      {
        "expected_failure": "The exact edit graph detects a wrong substitution distance.",
        "id": "M-substitution",
        "mutation": "Charge two edits for a substitution.",
        "observed_exit": 7,
        "receipt_sha256": "d2811c6d0585e523abe347041b5cf25f69a6145770b334089df15ba06cc09d4b"
      }
    ],
    "oracle_code": "import json, sys, itertools\nfrom collections import deque\n\ndef subsequences(word):\n    return {''.join(word[i] for i in range(len(word)) if mask & (1<<i)) for mask in range(1<<len(word))}\n\ndef distance(start,target):\n    if start==target: return 0\n    bound=max(len(start),len(target))\n    alphabet='01'\n    todo=deque([(start,0)]); visited={start}\n    while todo:\n        word,depth=todo.popleft()\n        if depth>=bound: continue\n        neighbors=set()\n        for pos in range(len(word)):\n            neighbors.add(word[:pos]+word[pos+1:])\n            for bit in alphabet: neighbors.add(word[:pos]+bit+word[pos+1:])\n        if len(word)<2*bound:\n            for pos in range(len(word)+1):\n                for bit in alphabet: neighbors.add(word[:pos]+bit+word[pos:])\n        for nxt in neighbors:\n            if nxt==target: return depth+1\n            if nxt not in visited:\n                visited.add(nxt); todo.append((nxt,depth+1))\n    raise ValueError('edit search exhausted')\n\ndef truth(batch):\n    answers=[]\n    for item in batch:\n        a,b=item['x'],item['y']; cap=item['N']; tag=item['star']\n        common=max(map(len,subsequences(a)&subsequences(b)))\n        pa=a+tag*(cap-len(a)); pb=b+tag*(cap-len(b))\n        pl=max(map(len,subsequences(pa)&subsequences(pb)))\n        q=max(len(a),len(b))-common\n        ed=distance(a,b); delta=len(a)+len(b)-2*common\n        if pl!=common+cap-max(len(a),len(b)) or cap-pl!=q: raise ValueError('padding identity')\n        if not q<=ed<=2*q or not ed<=delta<=2*ed: raise ValueError('metric comparison')\n        answers.append([common,q,pl,cap-pl,ed,delta])\n    return answers\n\nif __name__=='__main__':\n    try:\n        inp=json.load(open(sys.argv[1])); got=json.load(open(sys.argv[2])); wanted=json.loads(sys.argv[3])\n        exact=truth(inp)\n        sys.exit(0 if got==exact and wanted==exact else 7)\n    except Exception:\n        sys.exit(8)\n",
    "output_contract": "Exact rows giving original LCS, original subsequence deficit, padded LCS, padded deficit, unit-cost edit distance, and insertion–deletion distance.",
    "reason": "The fresh-symbol padding operation is a finite constructive component of the upper embedding. The code exercises its exact deficit identity and the finite-word metric comparisons; it does not implement or certify the asymptotic histogram embedding or either distortion obstruction.",
    "reference_code": "# INPUTS: JSON list of {x,y,N,star}; x,y are binary strings.\n# PRECONDITIONS: star is a fresh single character; max(len(x),len(y)) <= N <= 5; endpoint lengths <= 3; batch size <= 1000.\n# OUTPUTS: JSON rows [LCS,q,padded_LCS,padded_deficit,ED,insertion_deletion_distance].\n# TIME: O(sum(N*N)) exact table operations over the batch.\n# SPACE: O(N*N) table entries plus input/output storage.\n# ARITHMETIC: exact integer arithmetic using python-flint fmpz for deficits.\n# FAILURE: reject malformed or out-of-contract input with a nonzero exit.\nimport json, sys\nfrom flint import fmpz\n\ndef tables(a,b):\n    c=[[0]*(len(b)+1) for _ in range(len(a)+1)]\n    e=[[0]*(len(b)+1) for _ in range(len(a)+1)]\n    for i in range(len(a)+1): e[i][0]=i\n    for j in range(len(b)+1): e[0][j]=j\n    for i in range(1,len(a)+1):\n        for j in range(1,len(b)+1):\n            c[i][j]=c[i-1][j-1]+1 if a[i-1]==b[j-1] else max(c[i-1][j],c[i][j-1])\n            e[i][j]=min(e[i-1][j]+1,e[i][j-1]+1,e[i-1][j-1]+(a[i-1]!=b[j-1]))\n    return c[-1][-1],e[-1][-1]\n\ndef run(batch):\n    if not isinstance(batch,list) or not 1<=len(batch)<=1000: raise ValueError('batch')\n    result=[]\n    for row in batch:\n        if not isinstance(row,dict) or set(row)!={'x','y','N','star'}: raise ValueError('keys')\n        x,y,N,star=(row[k] for k in ('x','y','N','star'))\n        if not isinstance(x,str) or not isinstance(y,str) or any(c not in '01' for c in x+y): raise ValueError('words')\n        if max(len(x),len(y))>3 or type(N) is not int or not max(len(x),len(y))<=N<=5: raise ValueError('cap')\n        if not isinstance(star,str) or len(star)!=1 or star in x+y: raise ValueError('fresh')\n        l,ed=tables(x,y)\n        xp=x+star*(N-len(x)); yp=y+star*(N-len(y))\n        padded,_=tables(xp,yp)\n        q=int(fmpz(max(len(x),len(y)))-l)\n        deficit=int(fmpz(N)-padded)\n        delta=len(x)+len(y)-2*l\n        result.append([l,q,padded,deficit,ed,delta])\n    return result\nprint(json.dumps(run(json.load(sys.stdin)),separators=(',',':')))\n",

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | A finite histogram representation can supply a distance-preserving interface for bounded word data. A software implementation and its resource guarantees are ABSENT. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | A game or simulation model, update rule, and performance contract are ABSENT. |
| tools | True | weak | adaptable | inferred | Metric comparisons and padding identities can guide checks of word-distance representations. A packaged tool and implementation contract are ABSENT. |
| algorithms | True | strong | adaptable | inferred | The recursive overlapping-window construction defines a deterministic finite histogram map. Its distortion analysis does not provide an efficient running-time guarantee. |
| data_structures | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | Count histograms preserve occurrence multiplicity and expose distances through partition coordinates. Space and indexing complexity are ABSENT. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | An accelerator architecture, arithmetic format, and throughput or memory contract are ABSENT. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | A security model, adversary, and cryptographic guarantee are ABSENT. Binary substitutions here preserve word-distance deficits. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | A learned representation, training procedure, loss function, and prediction guarantee are ABSENT. The stated map is a deterministic mathematical construction. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | An operations-research objective, feasible-set model, and optimization algorithm are ABSENT. Distortion alone does not establish an optimization approximation guarantee. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | A distributed protocol, communication model, and consistency guarantee are ABSENT. |
| other (Metric geometry of word distances) | True | strong | inspirational | inferred | The lower constructions obstruct uniformly small distortion on the full capped word space, while the histogram construction supplies the matching upper scale. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **L1** (hypothesis): The metric counts single-symbol insertions, deletions, and substitutions at unit cost. Blocked use: ['Transferring the bounds directly to weighted edits, transpositions, or substitution-free distances without a separate comparison.']
- **L2** (edge_case): The length cap restricts comparison endpoints and includes the empty word; edit scripts may use longer intermediate words. Blocked use: ['Restricting intermediate words to the endpoint cap.', 'Excluding the empty word from the uniform upper claim.']
- **L3** (hypothesis): The uniform bounds concern finite alphabets with at least two symbols and sufficiently large integer caps. Blocked use: ['Applying the theorem to infinite alphabets, singleton alphabets, or caps below an unspecified starting threshold.']
- **L4** (counterexample_scope): The lower constructions supply hard binary subsets of one common length beneath the cap; their obstruction is to embedding the whole subset into the specified target. Blocked use: ['Claiming that every pair or every same-length subset has the lower distortion.', 'Transferring the obstruction to an arbitrary target space.']
- **L5** (approximation): The bounds determine the order of the logarithm of distortion; the exponent constants need not agree. Blocked use: ['Claiming an exact distortion formula or equality of the exponent constants.']
- **L6** (formal_scope): The embedding question places no restriction on target dimension or computation. A finite-dimensional deterministic map does not itself provide a runtime or memory guarantee. Blocked use: ['Inferring efficient evaluation, practical storage, or production performance from the distortion bound.']
- **L7** (hypothesis): The binary substitution lower bound is asserted only at equal input lengths; its upper bound holds for arbitrary finite words. Blocked use: ['Using the stated lower bound for unequal input lengths.', 'Assuming arbitrary binary encodings satisfy the proved transfer.']
- **L8** (nonconstructive): The direct code arguments fix codewords after showing that random choices have failure probability below one; the width is chosen sufficiently large. Blocked use: ['Treating an arbitrary sampled codebook or a prescribed small width as certified by the existence argument.']
- **L9** (hypothesis): The finite-circle comparison needs the stated frequency threshold for characters that annihilate the subgroup and detect the half-turn. Blocked use: ['Using the displacement inequality without establishing its arithmetic premise.']
- **L10** (hypothesis): The histogram partition distribution is fixed on the full finite domain before comparing input pairs, and list repetitions are counted. Blocked use: ['Replacing the deterministic expectation by an uncertified sample.', 'Choosing partitions separately for each pair.', 'Discarding list multiplicities.']
- **L11** (edge_case): Fresh-symbol padding requires a symbol absent from both original words. Common-suffix deficit preservation is stated for equal-length inputs. Blocked use: ['Using a nonfresh padding symbol in the fresh-padding identity.', 'Applying the common-suffix deficit formula outside its equal-length regime.']
- **L12** (formal_scope): The state-to-word maps may identify states; separated half-turn pairs do not imply global injectivity. Blocked use: ['Requiring every state image to be distinct or treating the state parametrization as an injective embedding.']
- **L13** (unknown): ABSENT: explicit universal thresholds, evaluated distortion constants, and an implementation complexity bound. Blocked use: ['Inventing a numerical starting cap or a concrete universal distortion coefficient.']

Then $$\\label{upper:histogram-map}\n \\mathcal H_\\alpha(L)=\\bigoplus_\\pi \\Pr[\\pi]\\,H_L^\\pi$$ is a deterministic finite-dimensional map whose distance equals $\\mathbb E\\|H_L^\\pi-H_{L'}^\\pi\\|_1$.",

If every cross-pair has $\\rho$-distance at least $a$, then $$\\label{upper:histogram-lower}\n T\\ge2s(1-s^2e^{-\\alpha a}).$$ If a pairing of list occurrences leaves $t$ occurrences unpaired on each side, then $$\\label{upper:coupling}\n T\\le2t+2\\sum_{(u,v)\\ {\\rm paired}}(1-e^{-\\alpha\\rho(u,v)})\n   \\le2t+2\\alpha\\sum_{(u,v)\\ {\\rm paired}}\\rho(u,v).$$ Writing $P_L^\\pi=H_L^\\pi/s$ and $\\operatorname{TV}(P,Q)=\\tfrac12\\|P-Q\\|_1$, the exact normalizations are $$\\label{upper:normalizations}\n \\|H_L^\\pi-H_{L'}^\\pi\\|_1\n =s\\|P_L^\\pi-P_{L'}^\\pi\\|_1\n =2s\\,\\operatorname{TV}(P_L^\\pi,P_{L'}^\\pi).$$",

<a id="pm-formal"></a>
### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 099 (`lean/docs/099.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [Edit Distance in $\ell_1$: Matching Bounds up to Constants in the Exponent](../../preprints/Edit-Distance-in-l1-Matching-Bounds-up-to-Constants-in-the-Exponent-September-27-2026/paper.pdf)
> - [Finite-Circle Obstructions, Binary Codes, and Histogram Embeddings for Edit Distance](../../preprints/Finite-Circle-Obstructions-Binary-Codes-and-Histogram-Embeddings-for-Edit-Distance-September-27-2026/paper.pdf)
> - [Tree Constructions for the $\ell_1$ Distortion of Binary Edit Distance](../../preprints/Tree-Constructions-for-the-l1-Distortion-of-Binary-Edit-Distance-September-27-2026/paper.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The formalization determines the exponential scale of the least $\ell_1$ distortion of unit-cost edit distance on strings of length at most $d$. For every sufficiently large $d$, uniformly over finite alphabets with at least two symbols, the distortion lies between $\exp(c\sqrt{\log d\,\log\log d})$ and $\exp(C\sqrt{\log d\,\log\log d})$ for absolute constants $c,C>0$. The lower bound has a witness consisting of binary strings of one common length, and the same scale controls the supremum over finite alphabets.
>
> The formalization gives two finite-circle constructions of binary strings whose least $\ell_1$ distortion is at least $\exp(c\sqrt{\log d\,\log\log d})$ for all sufficiently large length caps $d$. The witnesses use strings of one common length and include the binary coding transfers with their stated uniform bounds.
>
> It also gives a finite histogram embedding with distortion at most $\exp(C\sqrt{\log d\,\log\log d})$ for all strings of length at most $d$, uniformly over finite alphabets, including the empty string. Thus the lower and upper exponential scales agree up to absolute constants.
>
> The formalization gives two lower-bound constructions for the $\ell_1$ distortion of ordinary edit distance on binary words. For every sufficiently large length cap $d$, each construction supplies a finite set of at least two binary words of one common length at most $d$ whose least $\ell_1$ distortion is at least $\exp(c\sqrt{\log d\,\log\log d})$ for an absolute $c>0$.
>
> The selected statements are the binary lower bounds. The paper's constant-distortion binary conversion and the companion upper embedding theorem are outside them.

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

namespace FiniteCircle

open scoped BigOperators List

universe u v

inductive EditStep {α : Type u} : List α → List α → Prop
  | insert (u v : List α) (a : α) : EditStep (u ++ v) (u ++ a :: v)
  | delete (u v : List α) (a : α) : EditStep (u ++ a :: v) (u ++ v)
  | substitute (u v : List α) (a b : α) : EditStep (u ++ a :: v) (u ++ b :: v)

inductive EditScript {α : Type u} : ℕ → List α → List α → Prop
  | nil (x : List α) : EditScript 0 x x
  | cons {n : ℕ} {x y z : List α} :
      EditStep x y → EditScript n y z → EditScript (n + 1) x z

noncomputable def editDistance {α : Type u} (x y : List α) : ℕ :=
  sInf {n : ℕ | EditScript n x y}

noncomputable def lcs {α : Type u} (x y : List α) : ℕ :=
  sSup {n : ℕ | ∃ z : List α, z <+ x ∧ z <+ y ∧ z.length = n}

noncomputable def deficit {α : Type u} (x y : List α) : ℕ := x.length - lcs x y

abbrev Words (α : Type u) (d : ℕ) := {x : List α // x.length ≤ d}
abbrev RealL1 := lp (fun _ : ℕ => ℝ) 1

noncomputable def distortion {α : Type u} {X : Type v} (word : X → List α)
    (f : X → RealL1) : ℝ :=
  (sSup {r : ℝ | ∃ x y : X, x ≠ y ∧
    r = ‖f x - f y‖ / (editDistance (word x) (word y) : ℝ)}) *
  (sSup {r : ℝ | ∃ x y : X, x ≠ y ∧
    r = (editDistance (word x) (word y) : ℝ) / ‖f x - f y‖})

noncomputable def optimalDistortion {α : Type u} {X : Type v}
    (word : X → List α) : ℝ :=
  sInf {D | ∃ f : X → RealL1, Function.Injective f ∧ D = distortion word f}

noncomputable def E (α : Type u) (d : ℕ) : ℝ :=
  optimalDistortion (fun x : Words α d => x.val)

noncomputable def setDistortion (S : Set (List Bool)) : ℝ :=
  optimalDistortion (fun x : S => x.val)

noncomputable def alphabetSupremum (d : ℕ) : ℝ :=
  sSup {r | ∃ A : ℕ, 2 ≤ A ∧ r = E (Fin A) d}

noncomputable def exponentScale (d : ℕ) : ℝ :=
  Real.sqrt (Real.log d * Real.log (Real.log d))

def encode {α : Type u} {w : ℕ} (c : α → Fin w → Bool) (x : List α) : List Bool :=
  x.flatMap (fun a => List.ofFn (c a))

def interval {α : Type u} {w : ℕ} (c : α → Fin w → Bool)
    (a : α) (s len : ℕ) : List Bool :=
  ((List.ofFn (c a)).drop s).take len

def OffsetLocal {α : Type u} {w : ℕ} (δ : ℝ) (c : α → Fin w → Bool) : Prop :=
  ∀ a b : α, a ≠ b → ∀ s t : ℕ,
    s + w / 8 ≤ w → t + w / 8 ≤ w →
    (lcs (interval c a s (w / 8)) (interval c b t (w / 8)) : ℝ) ≤
      (1 - δ) * (w / 8 : ℕ)

def indexSpan {w : ℕ} (is : List (Fin w)) : ℕ :=
  match is with
  | [] => 0
  | i :: js => (js.getLastD i).val + 1 - i.val

def AnchorLocal {α : Type u} {w : ℕ} (ε : ℝ) (c : α → Fin w → Bool) : Prop :=
  ∀ a b : α, ∀ is js : List (Fin w),
    is.Pairwise (fun i j => i.val < j.val) →
    js.Pairwise (fun i j => i.val < j.val) →
    is.length = js.length → (w : ℝ) / 4 ≤ is.length →
    (indexSpan is - is.length : ℕ) ≤ ε * w →
    (indexSpan js - js.length : ℕ) ≤ ε * w →
    (∀ ij ∈ is.zip js, c a ij.1 = c b ij.2) →
    a = b ∧ ∃ i : Fin w, (i, i) ∈ is.zip js

def SubstitutionBounds {α : Type u} [Fintype α] {w : ℕ}
    (a C : ℝ) (c : α → Fin w → Bool) : Prop :=
  0 < w ∧ (w : ℝ) ≤ C * Real.log (2 * Fintype.card α) ∧
  (∀ L : ℕ, ∀ x y : List α, x.length = L → y.length = L →
    a * w * deficit x y ≤ (deficit (encode c x) (encode c y) : ℝ) ∧
    deficit (encode c x) (encode c y) ≤ w * deficit x y) ∧
  (∀ x y : List α, editDistance (encode c x) (encode c y) ≤ w * editDistance x y)

def TwoCodes (a C δ ε : ℝ) : Prop :=
  ∀ (α : Type) [Fintype α], 2 ≤ Fintype.card α →
    (∃ (w : ℕ) (c : α → Fin w → Bool),
      SubstitutionBounds a C c ∧ OffsetLocal δ c) ∧
    (∃ (w : ℕ) (c : α → Fin w → Bool),
      SubstitutionBounds a C c ∧ AnchorLocal ε c)

def period (h : ℕ) (p : ℕ → ℕ → ℕ) (l : ℕ) : ℕ := ∏ k : Fin h, p l k

def circleSize (h : ℕ) (p : ℕ → ℕ → ℕ) : ℕ :=
  2 * ∏ l : Fin h, period h p (l.val + 1)

def MaskPrimes (h : ℕ) (p : ℕ → ℕ → ℕ) : Prop :=
  (∀ l k : Fin h, Nat.Prime (p (l.val + 1) k)) ∧
  Function.Injective (fun lk : Fin h × Fin h => p (lk.1.val + 1) lk.2) ∧
  ∀ l k : Fin h, h ^ 3 ≤ p (l.val + 1) k ∧ p (l.val + 1) k ≤ 2 * h ^ 3

def ExcludedPrimes (m : ℕ) (p : ℕ → ℕ → ℕ) : Prop :=
  (∀ l k : Fin m, Nat.Prime (p (l.val + 1) k)) ∧
  Function.Injective (fun lk : Fin m × Fin m => p (lk.1.val + 1) lk.2) ∧
  ∀ l k : Fin m, m ^ 3 < p (l.val + 1) k ∧ p (l.val + 1) k ≤ m ^ 5

abbrev MaskTag (h : ℕ) (p : ℕ → ℕ → ℕ) (l : ℕ) := Σ k : Fin h, Fin (p l k)

def maskTags (h : ℕ) (p : ℕ → ℕ → ℕ) (l : ℕ) : List (MaskTag h p l) :=
  (List.ofFn fun k : Fin h => List.ofFn fun b : Fin (p l k) => Sigma.mk k b).flatten

def MaskAlphabet (h : ℕ) (p : ℕ → ℕ → ℕ) : ℕ → Type
  | 0 => Bool
  | l + 1 => MaskTag h p (l + 1) × MaskAlphabet h p l

instance maskAlphabetFintype (h : ℕ) (p : ℕ → ℕ → ℕ) (l : ℕ) :
    Fintype (MaskAlphabet h p l) := by
  induction l with
  | zero => exact inferInstanceAs (Fintype Bool)
  | succ l ih => exact @instFintypeProd _ _ inferInstance ih

def MaskState (h : ℕ) (p : ℕ → ℕ → ℕ) (M : ℕ) : ℕ → Type
  | 0 => ZMod M
  | l + 1 => MaskTag h p (l + 1) → MaskState h p M l

def shiftMaskState (h : ℕ) (p : ℕ → ℕ → ℕ) (M : ℕ) :
    (l : ℕ) → MaskState h p M l → ZMod M → MaskState h p M l
  | 0, v, θ => (show ZMod M from v) + θ
  | l + 1, v, θ => fun j => shiftMaskState h p M l (v j) θ

def maskGamma (h : ℕ) (p : ℕ → ℕ → ℕ) (M l a : ℕ)
    (j : MaskTag h p l) : ZMod M :=
  (∑ k : Fin h, ((M / p l k * (a % p l k) : ℕ) : ZMod M)) +
    ((j.2.val * (M / p l j.1) * (a % p l j.1) : ℕ) : ZMod M)

def maskWord (h : ℕ) (p : ℕ → ℕ → ℕ) (R M : ℕ) :
    (l : ℕ) → MaskState h p M l → List (MaskAlphabet h p l)
  | 0, v => (List.range M).map (fun b => decide (((b : ZMod M) - (show ZMod M from v)).val < M / 2))
  | l + 1, v => (List.range (R * period h p (l + 1))).flatMap fun a =>
      (maskTags h p (l + 1)).flatMap fun j =>
        (maskWord h p R M l
          (shiftMaskState h p M l (v j) (maskGamma h p M (l + 1) a j))).map
          (fun x => (j, x))

def ExcludedAlphabet (m L : ℕ) : ℕ → Type
  | 0 => Fin L × Bool
  | l + 1 => Fin m × ExcludedAlphabet m L l

instance excludedAlphabetFintype (m L l : ℕ) : Fintype (ExcludedAlphabet m L l) := by
  induction l with
  | zero => exact inferInstanceAs (Fintype (Fin L × Bool))
  | succ l ih => exact @instFintypeProd _ _ inferInstance ih

def ExcludedState (m L : ℕ) : ℕ → Type
  | 0 => ZMod L
  | l + 1 => Fin m → ExcludedState m L l

def shiftExcludedState (m L : ℕ) :
    (l : ℕ) → ExcludedState m L l → ZMod L → ExcludedState m L l
  | 0, v, θ => (show ZMod L from v) + θ
  | l + 1, v, θ => fun i => shiftExcludedState m L l (v i) θ

def excludedTranslation (m : ℕ) (p : ℕ → ℕ → ℕ) (L l s : ℕ) (i : Fin m) :
    ZMod L := ∑ j ∈ Finset.univ.erase i, ((L / p l j * (s % p l j) : ℕ) : ZMod L)

def excludedWord (m : ℕ) (p : ℕ → ℕ → ℕ) (R L : ℕ) :
    (l : ℕ) → ExcludedState m L l → List (ExcludedAlphabet m L l)
  | 0, v => List.ofFn fun b : Fin L =>
      (b, decide (((b.val : ZMod L) - (show ZMod L from v)).val < L / 2))
  | l + 1, v => (List.range (R * period m p (l + 1))).flatMap fun s =>
      (List.ofFn fun i : Fin m =>
        (excludedWord m p R L l
          (shiftExcludedState m L l (v i) (excludedTranslation m p L (l + 1) s i))).map
          (fun x => (i, x))).flatten

def MaskWitness (a C δ lower : ℝ) (d : ℕ) : Prop :=
  ∃ h : ℕ, 2 ≤ h ∧ ∃ p : ℕ → ℕ → ℕ, MaskPrimes h p ∧
    ∃ (w : ℕ) (c : MaskAlphabet h p h → Fin w → Bool),
      SubstitutionBounds a C c ∧ OffsetLocal δ c ∧
      ∃ n : ℕ, 0 < n ∧ n ≤ d ∧
        (∀ v : MaskState h p (circleSize h p) h,
          (encode c (maskWord h p (h ^ (2 * h)) (circleSize h p) h v)).length = n) ∧
        Real.exp (lower * exponentScale d) ≤ setDistortion
          (Set.range fun v : MaskState h p (circleSize h p) h =>
            encode c (maskWord h p (h ^ (2 * h)) (circleSize h p) h v))

def ExcludedWitness (a C ε lower : ℝ) (d : ℕ) : Prop :=
  ∃ m : ℕ, 2 ≤ m ∧ ∃ p : ℕ → ℕ → ℕ, ExcludedPrimes m p ∧
    ∃ (w : ℕ) (c : ExcludedAlphabet m (circleSize m p) m → Fin w → Bool),
      SubstitutionBounds a C c ∧ AnchorLocal ε c ∧
      ∃ n : ℕ, 0 < n ∧ n ≤ d ∧
        (∀ v : ExcludedState m (circleSize m p) m,
          (encode c (excludedWord m p (m ^ (2 * m)) (circleSize m p) m v)).length = n) ∧
        Real.exp (lower * exponentScale d) ≤ setDistortion
          (Set.range fun v : ExcludedState m (circleSize m p) m =>
            encode c (excludedWord m p (m ^ (2 * m)) (circleSize m p) m v))

def UniformUpper (C : ℝ) (d₀ : ℕ) : Prop :=
  ∀ (α : Type) [Fintype α], 2 ≤ Fintype.card α → ∀ d : ℕ, d₀ ≤ d →
    ∃ (M : ℕ) (F : Words α d → Fin M → ℝ), ∀ x y : Words α d,
      (editDistance x.val y.val : ℝ) ≤ ∑ i, |F x i - F y i| ∧
      (∑ i, |F x i - F y i|) ≤
        Real.exp (C * exponentScale d) * editDistance x.val y.val

def FullMain : Prop :=
  ∃ c C a Ccode δ ε : ℝ, ∃ d₀ : ℕ,
    0 < c ∧ 0 < C ∧ 0 < a ∧ 0 < Ccode ∧ 0 < δ ∧ 0 < ε ∧
    TwoCodes a Ccode δ ε ∧ UniformUpper C d₀ ∧
    (∀ (α : Type) [Fintype α], 2 ≤ Fintype.card α → ∀ d : ℕ, d₀ ≤ d →
      Real.exp (c * exponentScale d) ≤ E α d ∧
      E α d ≤ Real.exp (C * exponentScale d)) ∧
    (∀ d : ℕ, d₀ ≤ d →
      MaskWitness a Ccode δ c d ∧ ExcludedWitness a Ccode ε c d ∧
      Real.exp (c * exponentScale d) ≤ alphabetSupremum d ∧
      alphabetSupremum d ≤ Real.exp (C * exponentScale d))

end FiniteCircle

theorem finiteCircle_fullMain : FiniteCircle.FullMain := by
  sorry

end OAI

    "sha256": "c2bf47b24a5a09d8905878247f74e2bb9e785cbe6e449903aabf7dda60671fc4"

  "schema": "pm.formal.v2",
  "scope": {
    "doc": "lean/docs/099.md",
    "family": "099",
    "text": "The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):\n\n- [Edit Distance in $\\ell_1$: Matching Bounds up to Constants in the Exponent](../../preprints/Edit-Distance-in-l1-Matching-Bounds-up-to-Constants-in-the-Exponent-September-27-2026/paper.pdf)\n- [Finite-Circle Obstructions, Binary Codes, and Histogram Embeddings for Edit Distance](../../preprints/Finite-Circle-Obstructions-Binary-Codes-and-Histogram-Embeddings-for-Edit-Distance-September-27-2026/paper.pdf)\n- [Tree Constructions for the $\\ell_1$ Distortion of Binary Edit Distance](../../preprints/Tree-Constructions-for-the-l1-Distortion-of-Binary-Edit-Distance-September-27-2026/paper.pdf)\n\n## Scope\n\nThe formalization determines the exponential scale of the least $\\ell_1$ distortion of unit-cost edit distance on strings of length at most $d$. For every sufficiently large $d$, uniformly over finite alphabets with at least two symbols, the distortion lies between $\\exp(c\\sqrt{\\log d\\,\\log\\log d})$ and $\\exp(C\\sqrt{\\log d\\,\\log\\log d})$ for absolute constants $c,C>0$. The lower bound has a witness consisting of binary strings of one common length, and the same scale controls the supremum over finite alphabets.\n\nThe formalization gives two finite-circle constructions of binary strings whose least $\\ell_1$ distortion is at least $\\exp(c\\sqrt{\\log d\\,\\log\\log d})$ for all sufficiently large length caps $d$. The witnesses use strings of one common length and include the binary coding transfers with their stated uniform bounds.\n\nIt also gives a finite histogram embedding with distortion at most $\\exp(C\\sqrt{\\log d\\,\\log\\log d})$ for all strings of length at most $d$, uniformly over finite alphabets, including the empty string. Thus the lower and upper exponential scales agree up to absolute constants.\n\nThe formalization gives two lower-bound constructions for the $\\ell_1$ distortion of ordinary edit distance on binary words. For every sufficiently large length cap $d$, each construction supplies a finite set of at least two binary words of one common length at most $d$ whose least $\\ell_1$ distortion is at least $\\exp(c\\sqrt{\\log d\\,\\log\\log d})$ for an absolute $c>0$.\n\nThe selected statements are the binary lower bounds. The paper's constant-distortion binary conversion and the companion upper embedding theorem are outside them."
  },
  "status": "present",
  "toolchain": "leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1"
}
```
<!-- PM:END pm.formal.v2 -->

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We give two finite-circle constructions of binary strings whose least <i>ℓ</i><sub>1</sub> distortion is $`\exp(\Omega(\sqrt{\log d\,\log\log d}))`$, where <i>d</i> bounds their length. Both constructions supply words of one common length for every sufficiently large cap. Two direct binary coding arguments transfer the constructions with absolute distortion and logarithmic block width. We also develop the overlapping-substring method of Ostrovsky and Rabani into a complete finite histogram embedding at the same exponential scale, uniformly over all finite alphabets and all words of length at most <i>d</i>, including the empty word.
